     1→import {
     2→  useCallback,
     3→  useRef,
     4→  useState,
     5→  type ReactNode,
     6→} from 'react';
     7→import { AgentContext } from '../hooks/useAgent';
     8→import { runAgentLoop, runAgentEditLoop } from '../agent/agentService';
     9→import type { ChatMessage, ContentSegment, ToolCallResult } from '../types';
    10→import { useSessionId } from '../hooks/useSessionId';
    11→import { generateUUID } from '../utils/uuid';
    12→
    13→function generateId() {
    14→  return generateUUID();
    15→}
    16→
    17→export function AgentProvider({
    18→  children,
    19→  refreshTables,
    20→}: {
    21→  children: ReactNode;
    22→  refreshTables: () => Promise<void>;
    23→}) {
    24→  const userSessionId = useSessionId();
    25→  const [messages, setMessages] = useState<ChatMessage[]>([]);
    26→  const [isStreaming, setIsStreaming] = useState(false);
    27→  const abortRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
    28→  const textBufferRef = useRef('');
    29→  const flushTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
    30→  const assistantIdRef = useRef('');
    31→  const segmentsRef = useRef<ContentSegment[]>([]);
    32→  const currentTextRef = useRef('');
    33→  const phaseRef = useRef<'thinking' | 'answer'>('thinking');
    34→  const sessionIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
    35→  const pendingHistoryRef = useRef<{ role: string; content: string }[] | null>(null);
    36→
    37→  const flushText = useCallback(() => {
    38→    const text = textBufferRef.current;
    39→    if (!text) return;
    40→    const id = assistantIdRef.current;
    41→    currentTextRef.current += text;
    42→    setMessages((prev) =>
    43→      prev.map((m) =>
    44→        m.id === id ? { ...m, content: m.content + text } : m
    45→      )
    46→    );
    47→    textBufferRef.current = '';
    48→  }, []);
    49→
    50→  const sendMessage = useCallback(
    51→    async (text: string) => {
    52→      if (isStreaming) return;
    53→
    54→      const userMsg: ChatMessage = {
    55→        id: generateId(),
    56→        role: 'user',
    57→        content: text,
    58→      };
    59→
    60→      const assistantId = generateId();
    61→      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;
    62→      const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {
    63→        id: assistantId,
    64→        role: 'assistant',
    65→        content: '',
    66→        toolCalls: [],
    67→        isStreaming: true,
    68→      };
    69→
    70→      setMessages((prev) => [...prev, userMsg, assistantMsg]);
    71→      setIsStreaming(true);
    72→      textBufferRef.current = '';
    73→      segmentsRef.current = [];
    74→      currentTextRef.current = '';
    75→      phaseRef.current = 'thinking';
    76→
    77→      // Build conversation history from current messages for resume fallback
    78→      const history = messages
    79→        .filter((m) => m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant')
    80→        .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
    81→
    82→      // If there's pending history from a delete, start a new Langfuse session with that context
    83→      const pendingHistory = pendingHistoryRef.current;
    84→      pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
    85→      const langfuseSessionId = pendingHistory ? generateUUID() : null;
    86→
    87→      const controller = new AbortController();
    88→      abortRef.current = controller;
    89→
    90→      await runAgentLoop(
    91→        text,
    92→        sessionIdRef.current,
    93→        langfuseSessionId,
    94→        pendingHistory ?? (history.length > 0 ? history : null),
    95→        {
    96→          onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
    97→            textBufferRef.current += chunk;
    98→            if (!flushTimerRef.current) {
    99→              flushTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
   100→                flushText();
   101→                flushTimerRef.current = null;
   102→              }, 50);
   103→            }
   104→          },
   105→          onThinkingDone: () => {
   106→            // Extended thinking just ended and a text block is starting.
   107→            // Create a thinking segment from accumulated thinking text.
   108→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   109→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   110→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   111→            }
   112→            flushText();
   113→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   114→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'thinking', text: currentTextRef.current });
   115→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   116→            }
   117→            phaseRef.current = 'answer';
   118→            setMessages((prev) =>
   119→              prev.map((m) =>
   120→                m.id === assistantId
   121→                  ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   122→                  : m
   123→              )
   124→            );
   125→          },
   126→          onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {
   127→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   128→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   129→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   130→            }
   131→            flushText();
   132→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   133→              const segType = phaseRef.current === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   134→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: segType, text: currentTextRef.current });
   135→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   136→            }
   137→            // Add a pending tool segment so the input is shown immediately
   138→            segmentsRef.current.push({
   139→              type: 'tool',
   140→              toolResult: pending,
   141→            });
   142→            setMessages((prev) =>
   143→              prev.map((m) =>
   144→                m.id === assistantId
   145→                  ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   146→                  : m
   147→              )
   148→            );
   149→          },
   150→          onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {
   151→            // Merge result into pending tool segment (keep input info, add output)
   152→            const pendingIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   153→              (s) => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId
   154→            );
   155→            if (pendingIdx !== -1) {
   156→              const pending = segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx].toolResult!;
   157→              segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx] = {
   158→                type: 'tool',
   159→                toolResult: {
   160→                  ...pending,
   161→                  ...result,
   162→                  sql: result.sql || pending.sql,
   163→                  toolName: result.toolName || pending.toolName,
   164→                  command: result.command || pending.command,
   165→                  toolInput: result.toolInput || pending.toolInput,
   166→                },
   167→              };
   168→            } else {
   169→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: result });
   170→            }
   171→            setMessages((prev) =>
   172→              prev.map((m) =>
   173→                m.id === assistantId
   174→                  ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   175→                  : m
   176→              )
   177→            );
   178→            refreshTables();
   179→          },
   180→          onSubagentStart: (data) => {
   181→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   182→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   183→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   184→            }
   185→            flushText();
   186→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   187→              const segType = phaseRef.current === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   188→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: segType, text: currentTextRef.current });
   189→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   190→            }
   191→            segmentsRef.current.push({
   192→              type: 'subagent_start',
   193→              subagentId: data.id,
   194→              subagentName: data.name,
   195→              text: data.prompt,
   196→            });
   197→            setMessages((prev) =>
   198→              prev.map((m) =>
   199→                m.id === assistantId
   200→                  ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   201→                  : m
   202→              )
   203→            );
   204→          },

   533→
   534→  const deleteMessage = useCallback(
   535→    (messageIndex: number) => {
   536→      if (isStreaming) return;
   537→
   538→      // Store remaining messages as history for Langfuse context on next send
   539→      setMessages((prev) => {
   540→        const remaining = prev.slice(0, messageIndex);
   541→        pendingHistoryRef.current = remaining
   542→          .filter((m) => m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant')
   543→          .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
   544→        return remaining;
   545→      });
   546→
   547→      // Clear session so next message starts fresh
   548→      sessionIdRef.current = null;
   549→    },
   550→    [isStreaming]
   551→  );
   552→
   553→  const respondToQuestion = useCallback(
   554→    async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string) => {
   555→      // Update the segment to show the user's answer
   556→      const segIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   557→        (s) => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId
   558→      );
   559→      if (segIdx !== -1) {
   560→        segmentsRef.current[segIdx] = {
   561→          ...segmentsRef.current[segIdx],
   562→          userAnswer: answers,
   563→          userFreeText: freeText,
   564→        };
   565→        setMessages((prev) =>
   566→          prev.map((m) =>
   567→            m.id === assistantIdRef.current
   568→              ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   569→              : m
   570→          )
   571→        );
   572→      }
   573→
   574→      // POST to backend
   575→      try {
   576→        await fetch('/api/chat/respond', {
   577→          method: 'POST',
   578→          headers: {
   579→            'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   580→            ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   581→          },
   582→          body: JSON.stringify({
   583→            question_id: questionId,
   584→            answers,
   585→            free_text: freeText || null,
   586→          }),
   587→        });
   588→      } catch (e) {
   589→        console.error('Failed to respond to question:', e);
   590→      }
   591→    },
   592→    [userSessionId]
   593→  );
   594→
   595→  const clearMessages = useCallback(() => {
   596→    if (abortRef.current) {
   597→      abortRef.current.abort();
   598→    }
   599→    setMessages([]);
   600→    setIsStreaming(false);
   601→    sessionIdRef.current = null;
   602→    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   603→  }, []);
   604→
   605→  return (
   606→    <AgentContext.Provider
   607→      value={{ messages, isStreaming, sendMessage, editMessage, deleteMessage, clearMessages, respondToQuestion }}
   608→    >
   609→      {children}
   610→    </AgentContext.Provider>
   611→  );
   612→}
   613→

     1→import json
     2→import logging

     4→from starlette.applications import Starlette

     8→from mcp.server.sse import SseServerTransport
     9→from mcp.server.lowlevel.server import Server as MCPServer
    10→import mcp.types as types
    11→from app.session_manager import session_manager
    12→from app.database import Database
    13→from app.pending_questions import pending_question_store
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→MAX_RESULT_ROWS = 100
    18→
    19→# The path here is relative to the Starlette app mount point (/mcp), not the
    20→# full URL.  Starlette sets root_path=/mcp in the ASGI scope, and the MCP SDK
    21→# prepends root_path when advertising the endpoint URL to clients.  Using
    22→# "/messages/" avoids a double-prefix (/mcp/mcp/messages/).
    23→sse_transport = SseServerTransport("/messages/")
    24→
    25→
    26→def _create_mcp_server(db: Database, session_id: str) -> MCPServer:
    27→    """Create a duckdb-data-agent MCP server with execute_sql, ask_user_question, and render_chart tools bound to a DuckDB instance."""
    28→    server = MCPServer("duckdb-data-agent")
    29→
    30→    @server.list_tools()
    31→    async def list_tools() -> list[types.Tool]:
    32→        return [
    33→            types.Tool(
    34→                name="execute_sql",
    35→                description=(
    36→                    "Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database. "
    37→                    "Results are returned as JSON with columns, rows, and rowCount."
    38→                ),
    39→                inputSchema={
    40→                    "type": "object",
    41→                    "properties": {"sql": {"type": "string"}},
    42→                    "required": ["sql"],
    43→                },
    44→            ),
    45→            types.Tool(
    46→                name="ask_user_question",
    47→                description=(
    48→                    "Ask the user a clarifying question with selectable options. "
    49→                    "Pauses until the user responds."
    50→                ),
    51→                inputSchema={
    52→                    "type": "object",
    53→                    "properties": {
    54→                        "question": {"type": "string"},
    55→                        "options": {
    56→                            "type": "array",
    57→                            "items": {
    58→                                "type": "object",
    59→                                "properties": {
    60→                                    "label": {"type": "string"},
    61→                                    "description": {"type": "string"},
    62→                                },
    63→                                "required": ["label"],
    64→                            },
    65→                        },
    66→                        "multi_select": {"type": "boolean", "default": False},
    67→                    },
    68→                    "required": ["question", "options"],
    69→                },
    70→            ),
    71→            types.Tool(
    72→                name="render_chart",
    73→                description=(
    74→                    "Render a Plotly chart. Call this as the final step after querying data. "
    75→                    "Pass all Plotly traces in `data` and a layout object with a descriptive `title`."
    76→                ),
    77→                inputSchema={
    78→                    "type": "object",
    79→                    "properties": {
    80→                        "data": {
    81→                            "type": "array",
    82→                            "description": "Array of Plotly trace objects (bar, line, scatter, pie, etc.)",
    83→                            "items": {"type": "object"},
    84→                        },
    85→                        "layout": {
    86→                            "type": "object",
    87→                            "description": "Plotly layout object",
    88→                            "properties": {
    89→                                "title": {"type": "string"},
    90→                            },
    91→                            "required": ["title"],
    92→                        },
    93→                    },
    94→                    "required": ["data", "layout"],
    95→                },
    96→            ),
    97→        ]
    98→
    99→    @server.call_tool()
   100→    async def call_tool(
   101→        name: str, arguments: dict
   102→    ) -> list[types.TextContent]:
   103→        if name == "execute_sql":
   104→            sql = arguments.get("sql", "")
   105→            try:
   106→                result = await db.execute_query_async(sql)
   107→                truncated_rows = result["rows"][:MAX_RESULT_ROWS]
   108→                result_json = {
   109→                    "status": "success",
   110→                    "columns": result["columns"],
   111→                    "rows": truncated_rows,
   112→                    "rowCount": result["rowCount"],
   113→                }
   114→                return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps(result_json, default=str))]
   115→            except Exception as e:
   116→                error_json = {"status": "error", "error": str(e)}
   117→                return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps(error_json))]
   118→        elif name == "ask_user_question":
   119→            question_data = {
   120→                "question": arguments.get("question", ""),
   121→                "options": arguments.get("options", []),
   122→                "multi_select": arguments.get("multi_select", False),
   123→            }
   124→            question_id = pending_question_store.create(session_id, question_data)
   125→            answer = await pending_question_store.wait(session_id, question_id, timeout=300.0)
   126→            if answer is None:
   127→                result = {"timeout": True, "message": "User did not respond within the time limit."}
   128→            else:
   129→                result = answer
   130→            return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps(result))]
   131→        elif name == "render_chart":
   132→            data = arguments.get("data")
   133→            layout = arguments.get("layout", {})
   134→            if not isinstance(data, list) or not layout.get("title"):
   135→                logger.warning("render_chart called with missing data or layout.title: %s", arguments)
   136→                return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps({"status": "error", "error": "data and layout.title are required"}))]
   137→
   138→            # Reject specs where every trace has empty data arrays.
   139→            # Data-bearing fields vary by chart type (x, y, z, values, labels, etc.).
   140→            _DATA_FIELDS = ("x", "y", "z", "values", "labels", "lat", "lon", "r", "theta",
   141→                            "lowerfence", "q1", "median", "q3", "upperfence")
   142→            has_nonempty_trace = False
   143→            for trace in data:
   144→                if not isinstance(trace, dict):
   145→                    continue
   146→                for field in _DATA_FIELDS:
   147→                    val = trace.get(field)
   148→                    if isinstance(val, list) and len(val) > 0:
   149→                        has_nonempty_trace = True
   150→                        break
   151→                if has_nonempty_trace:
   152→                    break
   153→            if not has_nonempty_trace:
   154→                logger.warning("render_chart called with all-empty data traces: %s", arguments)
   155→                return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps({
   156→                    "status": "error",
   157→                    "error": "All data traces are empty — no data to chart. "
   158→                             "Check your SQL query results before calling render_chart."
   159→                }))]
   160→            # Return a minimal acknowledgement. The chart spec (data + layout) is captured
   161→            # upstream from the tool_use input block in the backend stream handler — not from
   162→            # this response — so we don't need to echo the payload here.
   163→            return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps({"status": "rendered"}))]
   164→        else:
   165→            raise ValueError(f"Unknown tool: {name}")
   166→
   167→    return server
   168→
   169→
   170→async def handle_sse(request: Request) -> Response:
   171→    """Handle SSE connection. Requires session_id query param."""
   172→    session_id = request.query_params.get("session_id")
   173→    if not session_id:
   174→        return Response("session_id query parameter is required", status_code=400)
   175→
   176→    db = session_manager.get_or_create(session_id)
   177→    server = _create_mcp_server(db, session_id)
   178→
   179→    try:
   180→        async with sse_transport.connect_sse(
   181→            request.scope, request.receive, request._send
   182→        ) as streams:
   183→            await server.run(
   184→                streams[0],
   185→                streams[1],
   186→                server.create_initialization_options(),
   187→            )
   188→    except anyio.ClosedResourceError:
   189→        logger.debug("MCP SSE client disconnected (session %s)", session_id)
   190→    except BaseExceptionGroup as eg:
   191→        # The MCP library uses anyio task groups which wrap errors in
   192→        # ExceptionGroups.  Filter out ClosedResourceError (normal client
   193→        # disconnect) and re-raise anything else.
   194→        _, rest = eg.split(anyio.ClosedResourceError)
   195→        if rest:
   196→            raise rest from None
   197→        logger.debug("MCP SSE client disconnected (session %s)", session_id)
   198→
   199→    return Response()
   200→
   201→
   202→mcp_app = Starlette(
   203→    routes=[
   204→        Route("/sse", endpoint=handle_sse, methods=["GET"]),
   205→        Mount("/messages/", app=sse_transport.handle_post_message),
   206→    ]
   207→)
   208→
```

All changes are implemented and verified. Here is a summary of what was done:

---

## Summary of Changes

### Problem
Charts were emitted to the UI via `subagent_end` events before the orchestrator streams its final answer. The orchestrator never saw chart data and could not coherently synthesize text with charts.

### Solution
Buffer chart specs from the chart-builder subagent, then emit them as `chart` SSE events just before the orchestrator's first answer text. The frontend handles these as `subagent_end` segments (with `chart_spec`) for rendering compatibility.

### Files Modified

#### 1. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/agent-a4699637/backend/app/mcp_sse.py` (line 160-163)
- Changed `render_chart` tool response from `{"status": "rendered"}` to `{"status": "success", "chart_spec": {"data": data, "layout": layout}}`
- This lets the chart-builder subagent see the chart spec in its tool result and describe the data in its final text output

#### 2. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/agent-a4699637/backend/app/agent.py`
- **Line 29**: Updated system prompt instruction to say "describe what the visualization shows based on the data. The chart will be displayed automatically before your answer text."
- **Lines 310-312**: Added `pending_charts`, `pending_subagents`, and `charts_emitted` tracking variables
- **Line 436**: Added `pending_subagents.add(tool_id)` when a Task subagent starts
- **Lines 375-380**: Before first answer text (when all subagents are done), emit buffered charts as `event: chart` SSE events
- **Lines 510-521**: When chart-builder subagent ends, buffer chart specs into `pending_charts` instead of including them in `subagent_end` data. Still emit `subagent_end` (without charts) so the frontend can close the "working..." indicator
- **Lines 552-556**: Safety net: emit any remaining buffered charts before `done` if they were not emitted during answer streaming

#### 3. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/agent-a4699637/frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts`
- **Line 10**: Added `onChart` callback to `AgentCallbacks` interface
- **Lines 258-266**: Added `chart` case in `handleSSEEvent` that extracts `chart_spec` and calls `callbacks.onChart`

#### 4. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/agent-a4699637/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`
- **Lines 221-233**: Added `onChart` callback in `sendMessage` that pushes a `subagent_end` segment with `chart_spec` (reusing existing type for rendering compatibility)
- **Lines 473-485**: Added identical `onChart` callback in `editMessage`

### Verification
- TypeScript type check passes (`npx tsc --noEmit`)
- Python syntax check passes for both modified backend files
- The `MessageBubble.tsx` rendering logic already handles `subagent_end` segments with `chart_spec` by rendering them via `ChartWidget`, so no changes needed there
- The chart segments from `onChart` intentionally omit `subagentId` and `subagentName` since the rendering code checks for `chart_spec` presence, not those fields

> AGENT

I've reviewed the Path 1 worktree diff. I found a few issues to fix before applying to main:

**Issues found:**

1. **Bug: `charts_emitted` flag prevents multi-chart-builder scenarios.** If the orchestrator calls chart-builder twice, the second batch of charts would never be emitted because `charts_emitted` is already `True`. Fix: remove the flag, just check `pending_charts` non-empty.

2. **Missing safety net on normal `done` path.** Pending charts are only emitted in the `not done_sent` error path. If the orchestrator sends `result` without streaming answer text_deltas first, charts are lost.

3. **`render_chart` returning full chart spec wastes tokens.** The chart-builder already constructed the spec — echoing all Plotly data arrays back to it is pointless token usage. Just return `{"status": "success"}`.

Let me fix these in the worktree and then apply to main.

> AGENT

All checks pass. Here's a summary of the Path 1 implementation:

### Changes (4 files)

**`backend/app/agent.py`** — Core change: buffer chart specs, emit in orchestrator's answer stream
- Added `pending_charts` (buffer) and `pending_subagents` (track active subagents)
- On `subagent_start`: add to `pending_subagents`
- On chart-builder `subagent_end`: buffer chart specs into `pending_charts` instead of emitting immediately; emit `subagent_end` without charts (closes "working..." indicator)
- On orchestrator answer text_delta: if `pending_charts` non-empty and no active subagents, emit buffered charts as `chart` SSE events before the text
- Safety nets: flush pending charts before `done` (both normal and error paths)
- Updated system prompt to tell orchestrator "chart will be displayed automatically before your answer text"

**`backend/app/mcp_sse.py`** — `render_chart` returns `{"status": "success"}` (was `"rendered"`)

**`frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts`** — New `onChart` callback + `chart` SSE event handler

**`frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`** — `onChart` creates `subagent_end` segment with `chart_spec` (reuses existing rendering in MessageBubble)

### Result
Charts now appear as part of the orchestrator's final answer stream, right before the synthesis text. The orchestrator has full context from the chart-builder's text output to write coherent commentary.

Would you like me to commit these changes?

> AGENT

Committed and pushed as `eb3afbcd` to `main`. The worktree can be cleaned up — the changes are now on main.

> DEVELOPER

I found Executing... is kept showing in thinking block under the render_chart tool call result, is it correct?

> AGENT

That's a bug. The render_chart `tool_call` event is emitted for the subagent-internal tool call, but the SDK likely doesn't yield the corresponding `tool_result` user message for subagent-internal tools — so "Executing..." never resolves.

The fix: don't emit `tool_call` events for subagent-internal tool_use blocks. The chart spec is already captured by `_build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages` separately.

> AGENT

The issue is at line 412-429: the `for block` loop emits `tool_call` for ALL tool_use blocks, including subagent-internal ones (render_chart, execute_sql inside chart-builder). The SDK doesn't yield the matching `user` message for these, so the frontend never gets a `tool_result` to resolve "Executing...".

Fix: skip `tool_call` emission for subagent messages, but still store `tool_names` for chart spec capture.

> AGENT

Good. The `tool_names[tool_id] = tool_name` still happens before the `continue` (line 419), so `_build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages` can still look up subagent tool names. But the `tool_call` event and all downstream UI-facing logic is skipped for subagent-internal messages.

Now let me also check: does `has_tool_calls` need to be set for subagent messages? It controls whether text_deltas are emitted as `answer` vs `thinking`. Since the orchestrator's own Task tool_use will set `has_tool_calls = True`, skipping it for subagent-internal tools is fine.

The issue was that subagent-internal tool_use blocks (render_chart inside chart-builder) were emitted as `tool_call` events, but the SDK never yields the matching `tool_result` for those. Now subagent-internal tool calls are silently tracked (for chart spec extraction) but not shown in the UI.

> AGENT

Now I can see the issue clearly. In line 884, the answer block structure is:

```
Answer
  [Chart 0]
  [Chart 1]
  [Chart 2]
  [Text: "Titanic survival — a compact data story..."]
```

All 3 charts are stacked at the top, then ALL the text follows. The user wants charts interleaved with their relevant text sections.

This is the fundamental limitation of the current approach: **all buffered charts are emitted in a single burst before the first answer text_delta**. The orchestrator has no way to signal "insert chart here" mid-stream.

The user wants something like:
```
Answer
  Text: "Survival by Sex and Class..."
  [Chart 1: Survival rate by Sex within Pclass]
  Text: "Age distribution..."
  [Chart 2: Age distribution box plot]
  Text: "Fare analysis..."
  [Chart 3: Fare distribution]
  Text: "Conclusion..."
```

This requires the orchestrator to explicitly reference charts at specific points in its answer. The orchestrator needs a way to emit chart markers inline with its text.

Let me rethink the approach. The orchestrator should call `render_chart` directly (this is essentially Path 2). That way charts appear naturally at the point the orchestrator decides to show them, interleaved with text.

> TOOL

    25→- When using the Task tool, you MUST set the "subagent_type" parameter to one of the named agents listed below. NEVER use generic values like "general-purpose", "code-writer", or any other value.
    26→- For any data question that requires SQL queries, set subagent_type to "sql-analyst"
    27→- For any visualization, chart, or graph request, set subagent_type to "chart-builder"
    28→- Do NOT set the "model" parameter on the Task tool — the named agents already have models configured. Omit the model field entirely.
    29→- After the chart-builder returns, describe what the visualization shows based on the data. The chart will be displayed automatically before your answer text.
    30→- After the sql-analyst returns, do NOT repeat the data tables or numbers. Simply add brief commentary on what the data means.
    31→- Explain findings in plain language after getting results
    32→
    33→Identity:
    34→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    35→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    36→
    37→Clarification:
    38→- When the user's request is ambiguous or could be interpreted in multiple ways, use the mcp__duckdb-data-agent__ask_user_question tool (NOT the native AskUserQuestion tool) to ask for clarification before proceeding.
    39→- Provide 2-4 clear, concise options for the user to choose from.
    40→- Each option should have a short label and optional description.
    41→- Only ask when genuinely needed — don't over-ask for trivial decisions.
    42→"""
    43→    if not tables:
    44→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    45→    else:
    46→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    47→        for table in tables:
    48→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    49→            for col in table["columns"]:
    50→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    51→
    52→    return prompt
    53→
    54→
    55→def _build_table_schemas(db: Database) -> str:
    56→    """Build a text description of all loaded table schemas for subagent prompts."""
    57→    tables = db.list_tables()
    58→    if not tables:
    59→        return "\nNo tables are currently loaded."
    60→    text = "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    61→    for table in tables:
    62→        text += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    63→        for col in table["columns"]:
    64→            text += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    65→    return text
    66→

   152→def _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(
   153→    msg: dict, tool_names: dict[str, str], subagent_chart_specs: dict
   154→) -> None:
   155→    """Inspect an assistant message and capture render_chart input as chart_spec.
   156→
   157→    When the chart-builder subagent calls render_chart, the tool_use block
   158→    appears in an assistant message whose parent_tool_use_id is the Task
   159→    tool_use_id for the chart-builder.  We extract input directly so we never
   160→    need to parse free-text JSON.
   161→
   162→    A single chart-builder may call render_chart multiple times (e.g. for a
   163→    multi-chart request).  We accumulate all specs in a list keyed by the
   164→    parent task tool_use_id.
   165→    """
   166→    parent_id = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   167→    if not parent_id or tool_names.get(parent_id) != "chart-builder":
   168→        return
   169→    _DATA_FIELDS = ("x", "y", "z", "values", "labels", "lat", "lon", "r", "theta",
   170→                     "lowerfence", "q1", "median", "q3", "upperfence")
   171→    for block in msg.get("message", {}).get("content", []):
   172→        if block.get("type") == "tool_use" and "render_chart" in block.get("name", ""):
   173→            spec = block.get("input", {})
   174→            # Skip specs with all-empty data traces to avoid rendering blank charts.
   175→            traces = spec.get("data", [])
   176→            has_data = any(
   177→                isinstance(trace.get(f), list) and len(trace.get(f)) > 0
   178→                for trace in traces if isinstance(trace, dict)
   179→                for f in _DATA_FIELDS
   180→            )
   181→            if not has_data:
   182→                return
   183→            subagent_chart_specs.setdefault(parent_id, []).append(spec)
   184→            return
   185→
   186→

   188→    message: str,
   189→    session_id: str | None = None,
   190→    db: Database | None = None,
   191→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None,

   506→            if pending_charts:
   507→                for chart_spec in pending_charts:
   508→                    yield f"event: chart\ndata: {json.dumps({'chart_spec': chart_spec}, default=str)}\n\n"
   509→                pending_charts.clear()
   510→            logger.warning("Sidecar stream ended without result message; sending done event")
   511→            yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': 'Connection to agent was lost. Please try again.'})}\n\n"
   512→            yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   513→
   514→    except Exception as e:
   515→        logger.error("Container agent error: %s", str(e))
```

> AGENT

Pushed as `1fdce66a`. To answer your question about sql-analyst: I kept it for complex multi-query data exploration (iterative SQL debugging, unfamiliar datasets). The orchestrator can now choose:

- **Simple queries**: call `execute_sql` directly
- **Complex exploration**: delegate to `sql-analyst` subagent
- **Charts**: call `execute_sql` + `render_chart` directly, interleaved with narrative text

The key difference from before: charts now appear **exactly where the orchestrator places them** in its answer, not grouped at the top. The orchestrator can write "Here's the survival rate by class:" → render chart → "As we can see..." → render next chart → conclusion.

     9→
    10→def parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    11→    """Parse 'real_model@sdk_alias' -> (sdk_alias, real_model).
    12→
    13→    If no '@', returns (value, value) for backwards compatibility.
    14→    """
    15→    if "@" in value:
    16→        real, sdk = value.rsplit("@", 1)
    17→        return sdk, real
    18→    return value, value
    19→
    20→
    21→def build_model_rewrites(pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> dict[str, str]:
    22→    """Build a rewrite map from (sdk_alias, real_model) pairs.
    23→
    24→    Only includes entries where sdk != real (i.e. rewriting is needed).
    25→    """
    26→    return {sdk: real for sdk, real in pairs if sdk != real}
    27→
    28→
    29→_raw_model = os.getenv("ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-4-6")
    30→_raw_sql = os.getenv("SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "inherit")
    31→_raw_chart = os.getenv("CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "inherit")
    32→
    33→ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_SDK, ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_model)
    34→SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_sql)
    35→CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_chart)
    36→
    37→MODEL_REWRITES = build_model_rewrites([
    38→    (ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_SDK, ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_REAL),
    39→    (SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL),
    40→    (CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL),
    41→])
    42→
    43→DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL = parse_model(os.getenv("DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL", ""))[1]
    44→
    45→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY", "")
    46→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY", "")
    47→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_BASE_URL", "https://cloud.langfuse.com")
    48→LANGFUSE_ENABLED = bool(LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY and LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY)

    50→PROJECT_DIR = os.getenv("PROJECT_DIR", os.getcwd())

    53→# Container isolation settings
    54→CONTAINER_IMAGE = os.getenv("CONTAINER_IMAGE", "duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest")
    55→CONTAINER_RUNTIME = os.getenv("CONTAINER_RUNTIME", "runc")
    56→CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT = os.getenv("CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT", "512m")

    11→
    12→def test_build_subagent_definitions_returns_two_agents():
    13→    """Should return sql-analyst and chart-builder AgentDefinitions."""
    14→    from app.agent import build_subagent_definitions
    15→
    16→    db = _make_db()
    17→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)
    18→
    19→    assert "sql-analyst" in agents
    20→    assert "chart-builder" in agents
    21→    assert isinstance(agents["sql-analyst"], AgentDefinition)
    22→    assert isinstance(agents["chart-builder"], AgentDefinition)
    23→
    24→
    25→def test_sql_analyst_has_execute_sql_tool():
    26→    from app.agent import build_subagent_definitions
    27→
    28→    db = _make_db()
    29→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)
    30→
    31→    assert agents["sql-analyst"].tools == ["mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql"]
    32→
    33→

    35→    from app.agent import build_subagent_definitions
    36→
    37→    db = _make_db()
    38→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)
    39→
    40→    assert agents["chart-builder"].tools == ["mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql", "mcp__duckdb-data-agent__render_chart"]
    41→
    42→
    43→def test_subagent_prompts_include_table_schemas():

    46→    db = _make_db(tables=[{

    57→

docs/plans/2026-02-25-render-chart-mcp-structured-output-design.md:8:The chart-builder subagent currently outputs a free-text response containing a JSON code block. The backend parses this with a regex-based `_extract_chart_spec()` function. This is fragile — if the LLM omits `layout.title` or formats the JSON differently, the chart either renders without a title or fails to parse entirely.
docs/plans/2026-02-25-render-chart-mcp-structured-output-design.md:12:Replace free-text chart output with a structured MCP tool call. The chart-builder must call `render_chart(data, layout)` where `layout.title` is required by the JSON schema. This makes the title mandatory at the schema level, not just a prompt instruction.

     1→"""Tests for render_chart tool_use capture in stream_chat message handling."""
     2→from app.agent import _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages
     3→
     4→
     5→def _make_assistant_msg(parent_tool_use_id, tool_use_name, tool_input):
     6→    """Helper: build a minimal SDK 'assistant' message dict."""
     7→    return {
     8→        "type": "assistant",
     9→        "parent_tool_use_id": parent_tool_use_id,
    10→        "message": {
    11→            "content": [
    12→                {
    13→                    "type": "tool_use",
    14→                    "id": "tu_render_1",
    15→                    "name": tool_use_name,
    16→                    "input": tool_input,
    17→                }
    18→            ]
    19→        },
    20→    }
    21→
    22→
    23→def test_captures_render_chart_input_as_chart_spec():
    24→    """render_chart tool_use.input is captured as chart_spec for the parent task id."""
    25→    chart_input = {
    26→        "data": [{"type": "bar", "x": ["A", "B"], "y": [1, 2]}],
    27→        "layout": {"title": "Sales by Category"},
    28→    }
    29→    msg = _make_assistant_msg(
    30→        parent_tool_use_id="task_001",
    31→        tool_use_name="mcp__duckdb-data-agent__render_chart",
    32→        tool_input=chart_input,
    33→    )
    34→
    35→    specs: dict = {}
    36→    tool_names = {"task_001": "chart-builder"}
    37→    _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg, tool_names, specs)
    38→
    39→    assert specs["task_001"] == [chart_input]
    40→
    41→
    42→def test_ignores_non_render_chart_tool_use():
    43→    """Other tool_use blocks in a chart-builder turn are not captured as chart_spec."""
    44→    msg = _make_assistant_msg(
    45→        parent_tool_use_id="task_001",
    46→        tool_use_name="mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql",
    47→        tool_input={"sql": "SELECT 1"},
    48→    )
    49→
    50→    specs: dict = {}
    51→    tool_names = {"task_001": "chart-builder"}
    52→    _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg, tool_names, specs)
    53→
    54→    assert specs == {}
    55→
    56→
    57→def test_ignores_messages_not_from_chart_builder():

    59→    msg = _make_assistant_msg(
    60→        parent_tool_use_id="task_002",
    61→        tool_use_name="mcp__duckdb-data-agent__render_chart",
    62→        tool_input={"data": [], "layout": {"title": "x"}},

    64→
    65→    specs: dict = {}
    66→    tool_names = {"task_002": "sql-analyst"}  # not chart-builder
    67→    _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg, tool_names, specs)
    68→
    69→    assert specs == {}
    70→
    71→
    72→def test_multiple_render_chart_calls_accumulated():
    73→    """Multiple render_chart calls by the same chart-builder are all captured."""
    74→    chart_input_1 = {
    75→        "data": [{"type": "bar", "x": ["A", "B"], "y": [1, 2]}],

    78→    chart_input_2 = {

    82→    msg1 = _make_assistant_msg(

    87→    msg2 = _make_assistant_msg(

    92→
    93→    specs: dict = {}
    94→    tool_names = {"task_001": "chart-builder"}
    95→    _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg1, tool_names, specs)
    96→    _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg2, tool_names, specs)
    97→
    98→    assert specs["task_001"] == [chart_input_1, chart_input_2]
    99→

   103→    chart_a = {

```
    28→
    29→### Agent Mode (default mode)
    30→
    31→- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the orchestrator delegates to specialized subagents that write and execute SQL for you
    32→- **Subagent architecture** — An orchestrator agent delegates to a **sql-analyst** subagent for data queries and a **chart-builder** subagent for visualizations, each with focused prompts and configurable models (via `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL` and `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL` env vars, defaulting to `haiku`)
    33→- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python); subagent internal reasoning is filtered from the main stream
    34→- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries
    35→- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation
    36→- **Chart generation** — Ask for a chart or visualization and the chart-builder subagent generates it inline; supports bar, scatter, line, pie, histogram, box, and heatmap chart types with optional multi-series grouping, powered by Plotly; animated charts with frames, sliders, and play/pause controls are also supported
    37→- **Edit & delete messages** — Hover over any user message to edit or delete it; editing re-sends the modified query with prior conversation as context, deleting rewinds the conversation to that point
    38→- **Bifrost LLM gateway** — A [Bifrost](https://github.com/maximhq/bifrost) gateway service manages API keys centrally and routes LLM requests to multiple providers; sidecar containers never have access to real API keys (see [Security](#security))
    39→- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and credentials are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API
    40→- **Container isolation** — Run each agent session inside a [gVisor](https://gvisor.dev/)-sandboxed Docker container for code execution sandboxing and multi-tenant isolation; read-only rootfs, all capabilities dropped, no host filesystem or Docker socket access (see [Container Isolation](#container-isolation))
    41→- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions
    42→
```

```
    72→Edit `backend/.env` and set your Anthropic API key:
    73→
    74→```
    75→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
    76→REDACTED           # optional, defaults to sonnet
    77→SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL=haiku           # optional, model for SQL analyst subagent (default: haiku)
    78→CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL=haiku         # optional, model for chart builder subagent (default: haiku)
    79→DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL=                # optional, fallback model for SDK built-in tools (see below)
    80→MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES=524288000      # optional, max upload size in bytes (default: 500 MB)
    81→```
    82→
    83→> `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL` are only needed for the AI agent. The SQL playground works without them, but both require the backend running.
    84→
    85→#### Per-Subagent Model Routing
    86→
    87→You can route each agent (orchestrator, SQL subagent, chart subagent) to a different LLM provider by using the `@suffix` syntax:
    88→
    89→```
    90→ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL=openai/gpt-4o@sonnet
    91→REDACTED@haiku
    92→REDACTED@haiku
    93→```
    94→
    95→The format is `real_model@sdk_alias`. The SDK alias (after `@`) is the model name the Claude Agent SDK sees; the real model (before `@`) is what the backend proxy rewrites it to before forwarding to Bifrost. This lets you use any provider Bifrost supports (OpenAI, Bedrock, etc.) while keeping the SDK configuration unchanged.
    96→
```

    29→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    30→async def test_call_tool_executes_sql_successfully(db):
    31→    """execute_sql tool returns correct columns and rows via the MCP handler."""
    32→    import mcp.types as types
    33→
    34→    server = _create_mcp_server(db, "test-session")
    35→    handler = server.request_handlers[types.CallToolRequest]
    36→    request = types.CallToolRequest(
    37→        method="tools/call",
    38→        params=types.CallToolRequestParams(
    39→            name="execute_sql",
    40→            arguments={"sql": "SELECT * FROM test_tbl ORDER BY id"},
    41→        ),
    42→    )
    43→    result = await handler(request)
    44→    content = result.root.content[0]
    45→    parsed = json.loads(content.text)
    46→    assert parsed["status"] == "success"
    47→    assert parsed["columns"] == ["id", "name"]
    48→    assert len(parsed["rows"]) == 2
    49→    assert parsed["rows"][0]["name"] == "alice"
    50→
    51→
    52→@pytest.mark.asyncio

    70→    assert "columns" in parsed
    71→    assert "rows" in parsed
    72→    assert "rowCount" in parsed
    73→    assert parsed["rowCount"] == 2
